================================================================
68. HUNGER SCALE x HERRING ENERGY: ROLLOUT EVIDENCE (Section 67
    follow-up). NO PARAMETER CHANGED YET.
================================================================

Purpose: before picking the knob for the porpoise energy-balance
failure (67.4), measure the two leading candidates in an actual
rollout instead of on paper. The candidates are
HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE 0.8 -> 0.9 and pelagic_fish
energy_content 6500 -> 7500 MJ/ton, and the four variants are
the 2x2 cross:

  A_current      scale 0.8   ec 6500   (as on disk)
  B_hunger09     scale 0.9   ec 6500
  C_herring7500  scale 0.8   ec 7500
  D_both         scale 0.9   ec 7500

68.1 Predicted requirement (why both changes, not one)

The gate in Section 67 is evaluated at a_eff = a, i.e. infinite
visible prey. Since a*h = 0.05*20 = 1 for porpoises on herring,
a_eff/a = B_vis/(1 + B_vis), so the gate condition
a_eff/a >= fc/fc_max translates into a minimum visible herring
biomass per cell:

  variant          fc_max   needed a_eff/a   B_vis needed
  B_hunger09        1.444        0.969          31 t/cell
  C_herring7500     1.406        0.996         231 t/cell
  D_both            1.667        0.840         5.3 t/cell

Prediction: each change alone is cosmetic (it moves net_eat above
zero but demands a herring density that does not occur), while
the combination is multiplicative and lands at flock density.

68.2 Tool

ab_test_hunger_energy.py (new, root, alongside the other
ab_test_*.py). Uses train.py's _ProbeEnvBuilder, so the world is
the deterministic inference world with the real impact maps -
identical to the probe rollout logged during training. Nothing is
written to YAML: HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE is monkey-patched on
lib.world.functional_group and energy_content is rewritten on the
FG params dict via a wrapper around load_project_config, both
before EcosystemEnvironment builds its static caches.

Two additions worth keeping:

  * Loss attribution. Every stage of the tick pipeline is wrapped
    and the per-FG biomass delta accumulated, so a decline can be
    attributed to predation vs. the growth/starvation term vs.
    the sub-threshold extinction sweep. This is what turned the
    porpoise diagnosis around (68.4).
  * --policy {uniform,greedy}. See 68.3 - the choice of surrogate
    policy dominates every other factor in the experiment.

68.3 METHODOLOGICAL TRAP: an untrained rollout divides pi_eat by
     ~11

With no policy loaded every logit is 0, so the masked softmax is
uniform over the ~11 valid actions and pi_eat for the best prey
is only about 1/11. The energy-balance gate is derived for
pi_eat = 1. A "policy-neutral" rollout therefore understates
realized intake by an order of magnitude, and EVERY decision
maker starves regardless of the parameters: with --policy uniform
pelagic_fish dies at t~1900-2060 and gadoids at t~2590-2950 in
all four variants. Such a run cannot answer a survival question.

Direct proof: placing the entire porpoise biomass in the 10
richest herring cells (144 t/cell) did not help at all - death at
t16 instead of t17, total herring intake 1.06 t where pi_eat = 1
would give ~12 t.

Hence --policy greedy: in every cell the whole population eats
the available prey with the highest max_intake_rate*energy_gain
and rests where nothing is available; no move actions. That is
the regime the gate assumes, and it also removes dispersal, the
movement cost and the extinction sweep, so the energy balance is
isolated from the spatial mechanics. Report both brackets; the
uniform run is the lower bound, greedy the upper bound.

68.4 Porpoises: the extinction sweep, not starvation

Per-stage attribution of the 40 t of porpoises, --policy uniform,
identical in all four variants (dead at t16-17):

  growth/starvation term   -14.9 t   (37%)
  extinction sweep         -25.1 t   (63%)
  predation on them          0.0 t
  total herring eaten      0.2-0.3 t over the whole life

40 t spread over 267 cells at spawn, and every move action splits
a cell four ways, so cells fall below
extinction_threshold_factor * min_split_biomass = 0.5 * 50 =
25 kg and are zeroed. The sweep bleeds ~3.6%/tick - more than the
2.2%/tick starvation rate derived in Section 66-67. So under
diffusion the porpoise failure is dominated by the sub-threshold
sweep (the min_split_biomass = 50 item open since Section 22),
NOT by the energy balance. The energy-balance fix is necessary
but not sufficient.

68.5 Results, --policy greedy, 3000 ticks, 60x60

Porpoise survival (tick of first zero) and lifetime herring
intake:

  variant          t_zero    herring eaten
  A_current           112          5.6 t
  C_herring7500       321         12.2 t
  B_hunger09          376         17.0 t
  D_both             1744         52.3 t

68.1 CONFIRMED, and more strongly than predicted: D is 15.6x
A on survival and 9.3x on intake, while each single change gives
only 2.9x / 3.4x. The effect is clearly superadditive - exactly
the multiplicative B_vis argument. Porpoises still die
eventually, because herring itself declines to near zero (68.6).

68.6 The herring risk was misdiagnosed

Section 67-era worry: the two changes add top-down pressure on
herring (four herring eaters get both a gate and an energy boost)
while herring's own budget only gets the gate, since it eats
zooplankton. The rollout does not support this.

  * Herring dies of ITS OWN starvation, not of predation.
    Cumulative pelagic_fish biomass delta, --policy greedy:
    growth -11441 t vs. predation -558 t. Predation is 5% of the
    loss (21% with --policy uniform). Herring collapses BOTTOM-UP
    because zooplankton collapses.
  * energy_content 6500 -> 7500 is dynamically almost a no-op.
    Variant C matches A to within 1% on every FG. The reason is
    the opposite of the assumed one: more MJ per ton of prey means
    LESS prey biomass has to be removed per unit of energy, so
    gadoid intake of herring even falls slightly (552.7 ->
    508.1 t). It buys porpoise margin without costing herring
    biomass.
  * mareld2.yaml has no seals and no seabirds. The "four herring
    eaters" are gadoids and porpoises only, so that part of the
    Section 67 risk analysis did not apply to this project at all.

68.7 The real risk: the hunger scale amplifies the phyto-zoo
     overshoot

scale 0.9 makes every FG eat harder at a given satiation, and the
bottom of the web is where that bites. --policy greedy:

  quantity                 A (0.8)      B/D (0.9)
  zooplankton peak         1.67e5       1.81e5    (higher)
  zooplankton peak tick       634          492    (earlier)
  zooplankton trough        470.6        333.3    (deeper)
  zooplankton end          9596 t       3475 t    (0.36x)
  phytoplankton trough    21838 t      15312 t    (0.70x)

Classic overshoot-crash amplification: zooplankton grazes phyto
down harder and faster, then crashes deeper, and herring starves
in the trough (pelagic_fish reaches exactly 0 at t2796/2874 under
0.9 vs. 0.1 t remaining under 0.8). Note the sign flip between
brackets - with --policy uniform, scale 0.9 makes zooplankton
5.0x BETTER at t3000 - which is another reason not to read too
much into either bracket alone.

tests/test_phyto_zoo_sanity.py passes with the scale
monkey-patched to 0.9 (4 passed), so the change does not break
the documented phyto-zoo contract; the effect above is a
quantitative shift in the oscillation, not a broken invariant.

68.8 Conclusions and what is NOT decided

  1. The 0.9 + 7500 combination works as intended and is the
     only variant that materially helps porpoises. The
     multiplicative argument in 68.1 is empirically confirmed.
  2. energy_content 7500 is nearly free: no herring cost, real
     porpoise benefit. It is the safer half of the pair.
  3. HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE 0.9 is the half that carries the
     risk, and the risk is NOT herring predation - it is
     amplification of the phyto-zoo overshoot at the base of the
     web.
  4. Neither variant produces a persistent system under either
     surrogate policy. That is a pre-existing baseline problem
     (no trained policies exist post-Holling-fix, and there is
     still no seed_rate floor for DM species - P1), not something
     caused by these two changes.
  5. The porpoise fix ALSO needs the sub-threshold sweep
     addressed (68.4): min_split_biomass = 50 with
     extinction_threshold_factor = 0.5 costs more biomass than
     starvation does once the population diffuses.

NO parameter was changed IN THIS SECTION. SUPERSEDED by Section
69, which applied a per-FG variant instead of the global
D_both: pelagic_fish energy_content 6500 -> 7500 plus
porpoises satiation_scale 0.9, with HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE left
at 0.8 as the default for everyone else. The tables above were
measured BEFORE the sub-threshold split fix (69.1) existed and
are not directly comparable to later runs; use --variants
A0_prefix to reproduce this movement path.

68.9 Reproduce

  python3 ab_test_hunger_energy.py --ticks 3000 --policy greedy
  python3 ab_test_hunger_energy.py --ticks 3000 --policy uniform
  (~30 s per variant at 60x60; --variants selects a subset.)

================================================================
69. APPLIED: SUB-THRESHOLD SPLIT SUPPRESSION + HERRING ENERGY +
    PER-FG SATIATION SCALE (Section 68 follow-up)
================================================================

Three changes, decided on the Section 68 rollout data. Steps 1-3
of the proposal, plus the verification run.

  1. STRUCTURAL: movement splits may no longer create cells below
     the extinction threshold.
  2. PARAMETER: pelagic_fish energy_content 6500 -> 7500 MJ/ton.
  3. PARAMETER: satiation_scale becomes PER-FG (default 0.8);
     porpoises get 0.9. NOT the global 0.8 -> 0.9 of variant
     D_both.

Why per-FG and not global: Section 68 showed the benefit of 0.9
is concentrated in ONE FG (porpoises, whose ceiling/Rest_X
headroom is 3.25) while the damage is concentrated in another
(zooplankton/phytoplankton overshoot, 68.7). The stated argument
for going global - "it preserves the relative calibration between
species" - does not survive inspection: the headroom column spans
3.25 to 92.9, a factor of 28, so there is no cross-species
calibration to preserve. It is an accident that every FG carried
the same constant. Physiologically per-FG is also the more
correct reading: 0.8 means "stops eating when the reserve is 80%
full", and a blubber-dependent animal in temperate water tops up
further before it stops hunting than a copepod does. It is the
same kind of species-specific parameter maintenance_level already
is.

69.1 Change 1: _suppress_subthreshold_splits

lib/environments/ecosystem.py. A move action splits the moving
share of a cell over up to 4 directions; _apply_extinction_
threshold then zeroes every cell ending the tick below
thr = extinction_threshold_factor * min_split_biomass. For
porpoises (50 kg * 0.5 = 25 kg/cell) a diffusing population bled
biomass through that sweep faster than through starvation
(68.4: 25.1 of 40 ton to the sweep, 14.9 ton to starvation).

The variant chosen (the user's pick of the two proposed): an
outflow whose DESTINATION would still end up below thr after
receiving it is CANCELLED and returned to the source cell. Not
"lower min_split_biomass" - the threshold semantics are kept.

  * New cache _dm_split_thr / _dm_split_thr_any in
    _build_static_caches, reading the SAME product the sweep
    applies, so the two cannot drift apart.
  * The correction runs at the end of _apply_movement, after the
    migration immigration has been added, so edge cells are
    judged on their true post-tick total. Off-grid directions are
    treated as unblocked - migration keeps its own top-k
    concentration path (unchanged).
  * Destination totals are read per direction with the same
    slice offsets as the forward transfer, then subtracted with
    the sign flipped. Mass-conserving by construction.

Why a single pass is enough (this is the key property): the only
cells that lose inflow are cells that were below thr WITH all
inflows applied - i.e. cells the sweep was going to zero anyway.
Viable destinations keep every inflow, and source cells only
gain. Therefore the number of sub-threshold cells cannot
increase and retained biomass cannot decrease. The fix strictly
dominates the old behaviour for every FG, which is why it needed
no per-species opt-in. thr = 0 (all continuous FGs) disables it,
matching the sweep's own opt-out.

69.2 Change 2 + 3: the parameters

  fgconfig/fg_library.yaml
    pelagic_fish.energy_content   6500.0 -> 7500.0
    porpoises.satiation_scale     (absent) -> 0.9

mareld2.yaml needed NO edit: it carries only group_id and
biomass ranges. Every FG parameter comes from
fgconfig/fg_library.yaml, so there is no project/library drift
risk here (a claim made earlier in this arc that turned out to
be wrong).

  lib/world/energy_balance.py
    HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE is now the DEFAULT, not the value.
    New resolve_satiation_scale(value): None / "" / <= 0 /
    unparseable -> the default, so legacy FGs are bit-identical.
    hunger_at(s_x, scale=None) and evaluate_energy_balance(...,
    hunger_scale=None) both route through it.
  lib/world/functional_group.py
    self.satiation_scale = params.get('satiation_scale', None),
    resolved at every get_hunger() call (NOT cached in __init__)
    so a test or A/B script can patch the module default in
    place.
  fgconfig/fgconfig.py
    New DM field "Satiation Scale (hunger gate, 0=default 0.8)".
    0 / empty is stripped from the saved config rather than
    persisted as 0.0, and the value is threaded into
    evaluate_energy_balance(hunger_scale=...) so the GUI gate
    evaluates the same h(u_X) the runtime will.

Resulting porpoise balance:

    ceiling  = 0.05 * 7500 * 0.9      = 337.500
    h(u_X)   = 1 - 0.5/0.9            =   0.444
    realized = 0.4444 * 337.5         = 150.000
    feed     = 1.40 * 90              = 126.000
    net_eat                           = +24.000
    max allowed feeding_cost          =   1.667
    required B_vis (a_eff/a >= 0.84)  = 5.3 t/cell

feeding_cost stays 1.40, u_X stays 0.50, ME_X stays 2800,
resting_metabolism and max_intake_rate untouched. No Section 36
literature ceiling and no Section 46 starvation anchor is
touched: the hunger gate does not enter
tools/starve_calibration.py, which drains rm * resting_cost from
s_x(0) = u_X.

69.3 Verification rollout (ab_test_hunger_energy.py)

Two new variants: E_perfg (what was applied) and A0_prefix
(split_fix=OFF, i.e. the pre-69 movement path) so the structural
fix can be isolated. Note that A_current now INCLUDES change 1,
so the A-D rows are no longer directly comparable to the
Section 68 tables. Each variant also resets every per-FG
satiation_scale before applying its own, so a row means the same
thing before and after a fix is committed to the library.

--policy greedy (pi_eat = 1, no move actions at all):

  variant         porpoise t_zero   herring end   zoo end/B0
  A0_prefix              112          0.1 t         0.274
  A_current              112          0.1 t         0.274
  B_hunger09             376          0 (t2796)     0.099
  C_herring7500          321          0.1 t         0.273
  D_both                1744          0 (t2874)     0.100
  E_perfg               1088          0.1 t         0.274

Porpoise herring intake over the rollout: 5.6 t (A) -> 45.8 t
(E_perfg) -> 52.3 t (D_both).

THE DECISIVE RESULT: E_perfg gives porpoises 9.7x the survival
of the baseline (112 -> 1088 ticks) while leaving zooplankton at
0.274 (identical to baseline) and herring alive. D_both buys
another 60% of porpoise survival but DRIVES HERRING EXTINCT at
t2874 and cuts zooplankton to 0.100. Choosing per-FG was not a
cosmetic refinement - the global variant kills the porpoises'
own prey species. That was visible in 68.7 as an overshoot
amplification; here it shows up as an extinction.

--policy uniform (pi_eat ~ 1/11, the pessimistic bracket):

  variant       gadoids t_zero   herring t_zero   porpoise t_zero
  A0_prefix          2585            1989              16
  A_current          -  (alive)      2699              19
  E_perfg            -  (alive)      2613              20
  D_both             -  (alive)      -                 20

  porpoise loss attribution (ton, of 40 initial):
                    growth(starv)   extinct(sweep)
  A0_prefix             -14.9           -25.1
  A_current             -30.1            -9.9

This is the honest reading of change 1. Per-FG sweep loss:
porpoises 25.1 -> 9.9 t (-61%), gadoids 5.4 -> 0.8 t, herring
2.9 -> 0.2 t. Global persistence improves measurably: gadoids go
from extinct at t2585 to alive at t3000, herring from t1989 to
t2699 (+36%). But for porpoises in THIS bracket the retained
biomass merely starves instead of being swept (total still 40 t,
t_zero 16 -> 19), because a uniform forager cannot break even at
all - see 68.6. And in the greedy bracket the fix is invisible
because that surrogate never emits a move action.

So: change 1 is worth real biomass for every FG with an
indivisible weight, and it is provably never harmful (69.1), but
its benefit for porpoises specifically cannot be measured until
a policy exists that BOTH moves and forages competently. Neither
surrogate does.

69.4 Tests

  tests/test_split_threshold.py (new, 9 tests). Six contracts:
    1. Retention: a four-way split entirely below thr keeps all
       its biomass instead of being swept to zero.
    2. The pre-fix behaviour, asserted explicitly (whole
       population lost, 4 extinction events) so the test cannot
       go vacuous.
    3. No suppression when viable - ordinary dispersal untouched.
    4. Viability is judged on the destination TOTAL: the same
       small outflow is blocked into an empty cell and allowed
       into an already-populated one. This is what makes the rule
       "do not CREATE sub-threshold cells" rather than "do not
       send small amounts".
    5. Mass and reserve conservation (metabolic costs off).
    6. Monotone dominance over the pre-fix path on 25 random
       states: never less biomass, never more sub-threshold
       cells.
    Plus thr-cache == sweep bound, and the thr = 0 opt-out.

  tests/test_energy_balance_gate.py: 15 -> 22 tests.
    * resolve_satiation_scale fallbacks and overrides.
    * get_hunger uses the FG's OWN scale (the coupling that
      makes the gate meaningful at all).
    * h(u_X) monotone in the scale, so the knob has an
      unambiguous sign.
    * the 69.2 porpoise numbers pinned (net_eat +24.000,
      max_fc 1.6667).
    * porpoises must CARRY a scale override on the live project -
      guards against reverting the library only.
    * NEW GATE: every live DM needs net_eat > 5% of feed_cost.
      Rationale: the gate assumes a_eff = a, so a margin of
      +0.56 (what energy_content 7500 alone gives) demands ~231 t
      of visible herring per cell, which never occurs, while
      +24.0 demands ~5.3 t - one school cell. A sign-only gate
      would have accepted variant C.
    * _balance_for now threads hunger_scale from the FG params;
      without it the suite went green on the +0.56 margin, i.e.
      for the wrong reason. Worth remembering: a structural test
      that does not read the same parameter the runtime reads can
      pass for a reason that does not exist.

TRAP WORTH REMEMBERING (cost an hour): in a hand-built
_apply_movement test the action distribution MUST sum to 1 per
cell. pi_rest + pi_eat + pi_move is the only biomass carried
over; anything unallocated is silently deleted. Edge cells need
rest padding because move_mask zeroes off-grid directions.

69.5 Verification commands

  python3 -m pytest tests/ -q
    -> 104 passed, 0 failed  (88 before + 9 split + 7 gate).
       The Section 67.4 intentional red is now GREEN.
  python3 -m py_compile lib/environments/ecosystem.py
    lib/world/energy_balance.py lib/world/functional_group.py
    fgconfig/fgconfig.py -> OK
  python3 ab_test_hunger_energy.py --ticks 3000 --policy greedy
  python3 ab_test_hunger_energy.py --ticks 3000 --policy uniform
    (~25 s per variant at 60x60)
  python3 train.py --project mareld2.yaml --species porpoises
    --generations 1 --iter-per-gen 1 --workers 1
    --run-name sec69_smoke < /dev/null
    -> completes. Probe reports "porpoises: 22%" against the
       "porpoises: 2%" recorded in 67.5 for the same command,
       i.e. 11x the surviving biomass at the end of the probe
       rollout. Loss is still "starv 100% / pred 0%", as
       expected: the porpoise now loses ground more slowly but
       an untrained policy still cannot forage. The throwaway
       results/sec69_smoke/ checkpoint was deleted afterwards.

ALL CHECKPOINTS ARE INVALIDATED by changes 1-3 (the hunger gate
sits in the intake for porpoises, the movement operator changed
for every FG with an indivisible weight, and herring energy
enters both energy_gain_mat and the reward). This is not a new
cost: mareld2 policies had to be retrained after the Holling fix
anyway (64.6/64.7). Nothing was committed.

69.6 What is still open

  * P1: seed_rate for DM species. 68.8 point 4 still stands -
    NO variant produces a persistent system in either bracket.
    The recolonisation floor exists only for NDMs, and it is the
    point that directly addresses "prey never recovers". Without
    it, any retraining teaches the policies to live in a dying
    world.
  * Retrain the mareld2 reference policies (the three DMs in
    results/<run-name>/). Only after that can anything be said
    about long-term persistence: the uniform and greedy brackets
    gave OPPOSITE signs for zooplankton under scale 0.9 (5.0x
    better vs. 0.36x worse), so neither is a substitute for a
    real policy. Steps 1-3 were decidable on current data only
    because they concern energy balance at u_X and biomass
    accounting, both policy-independent.
  * starve_rate / growth_rate asymmetry (500x for porpoises,
    30x seals, 6x herring, 4.8x gadoids). Real, worth its own
    section: it makes every starvation dip effectively
    irreversible (~1400 ticks to recover). It aggravates
    recovery, it does not create the defect, and it was
    deliberately kept out of this change set.
  * Spatial mismatch at spawn (herring colony sigma=1 vs.
    porpoise sigma=3): most porpoise biomass still starts in
    cells with zero herring. Unaffected by any of the above.
  * Still open from 63.7: GUI validator warning when
    handling_time != 1/max_intake_rate.

================================================================
SECTION 70 - REFERENCE-GRID SPAWN INVARIANCE (--grid 16*16)
================================================================

Trigger: a training run

  python3 train.py --project mareld2.yaml --run-name
    pzbpgp248nsrpfix --migration on --mortality on
    --generations inf --profile info --iter-per-gen 50
    --n_eval_ticks 1000 --n_deltas 16 --grid 16*16
    --worlds_refresh iteration --policynetwork 2 48 tanh
    --lr 0.03 --sigma 0.05 --visual

reported that porpoises still die immediately, despite Section
69. They do - but not because Section 69 failed. On a 16x16 grid
there was literally NO cell in the entire world where a porpoise
could break even.

70.1 Mechanism

mareld2.yaml declares reference_grid_width/height = 60, and
config_loader scales every FG's initial TOTAL biomass by

  biomass_scale = (H_act * W_act) / (ref_H * ref_W)

For 16x16 that is 256/3600 = 0.0711, i.e. 14x less biomass.
cell_size is hard-coded to 1000 m in train.py, so the cells are
the same physical size - the WORLD is smaller, not coarser.
Density per cell should therefore be unchanged, and for uniform
/ env_driven FGs it is (zooplankton median 9.04 t/cell at 60x60
vs. 9.19 at 16x16).

But pelagic_fish and gadoids use mode: colony with
n_colonies: 25, and the colony COUNT did not scale with the
area. You still got 25 herring schools - sharing one fourteenth
of the fish.

  grid    herring total   max t/cell   cells where a porpoise
                                       can break even
  60x60    7700-23100      103-305      151-330 of 3600
  16x16      551-1647     10.9-32.5       0 of 256 (4/5 seeds)

(Measured over spawn seeds 1-5 at actual env construction.)

The Section 69 requirement is a_eff/a >= 0.84, and since
a*h = 0.05*20 = 1 for porpoise->herring, a_eff/a = B_vis/(1+B_vis),
i.e. 5.3 t of VISIBLE herring per cell. Herring has
visibility_floor = 0.35, so the best 16x16 cell (10.92 t) shows
3.82 t -> a_eff/a = 0.79. Below threshold, in the best cell on
the whole map. At 60x60 the same cell is 103 t -> 36 t visible
-> 0.973.

70.2 Two defects in lib/spawn/strategies.py

Defect 1 - the invariance mechanism was DEAD CODE.
_apply_grid_scaling reads context['biomass_scale'], but
config_loader._spawn_biomass_distribution was only ever passed
{'env_fields', 'allowed_mask'}. The key had never existed.
Verified by spying on the call: context keys seen were
['allowed_mask', 'env_fields'] on every invocation.

Defect 2 - the rule was WRONG even if it had fired. It scaled
`scale` and `sigma_cells` by sqrt(biomass_scale) and left
n_colonies alone. Under constant cell_size a colony of
sigma_cells = 1 is the same PHYSICAL size on every grid, so the
lengths must not be touched; what is extensive in area is the
COUNT of colonies. The sqrt rule on sigma is only correct if the
reference grid covers a fixed physical domain, i.e. if cell_size
varied with the grid - it does not.

70.3 The fix

  1. config_loader (load_project_config) now passes
     'biomass_scale': biomass_scale in the spawn env_context.
     The library-only path setup_full_mareld_mvp deliberately
     does NOT: it has no project reference grid, so total_b is
     not area-scaled there either, and the colony count must
     track whatever the biomass does.
  2. _apply_grid_scaling now scales n_colonies by biomass_scale
     (area), floored at 1, and returns early when
     biomass_scale == 1.0 so the reference grid is bit-for-bit
     preserved.
  3. The sqrt scaling of `scale` / `sigma_cells` is REMOVED.
     Since it had never been active, no existing run changes
     behaviour because of this.

Effect on herring spawn (seeds 1-5, cells clearing the 0.84
requirement WITH the visibility floor applied):

  BEFORE  16x16: max/cell = 11, 13, 14, 33, 13
                 viable   =  0,  0,  0, 37,  0
  AFTER   16x16: max/cell = 72, 64, 66, 276, 84
                 viable   = 15, 14, 18,  19, 10
  60x60 (control, unchanged): viable = 177, 175, 182, 330, 151

As a fraction of the world: 4.2-9.2 % at 60x60, 3.9-7.4 % at
16x16, 4.2-9.3 % at 30x30. The invariant is restored.

70.4 tests/test_spawn_grid_invariance.py (new, 24 tests)

Six contracts, same kind of structural guard as Section 66
(Holling) and 67 (energy balance):

  1. Wiring - the real loader puts biomass_scale in the spawn
     context, and its value is the area ratio. This is the one
     that catches Defect 1.
  2. Count scales with area, floored at 1, monotone.
  3. sigma_cells / scale come out EXACTLY as configured. Direct
     regression guard against the old sqrt rule.
  4. biomass_scale == 1.0 is a no-op (60x60 untouched); missing
     context, non-colony params and an unparseable n_colonies
     are all no-ops rather than raising.
  5. Density invariance, synthetic: same colony spec, same
     fraction of cells above a given t/cell, and a comparable
     peak density.
  6. Density invariance, live project: the fraction of cells
     where a porpoise clears the Section 69 requirement is
     within a factor of 2 between 16x16 and 60x60, and is
     nonzero on every seed.

Verified to FAIL against the pre-fix behaviour (the no-op
_apply_grid_scaling that was actually running):
  contract 6 -> viable fractions [0, 0, 0, 0.145, 0]
  contract 5 -> peak 15.4 t/cell vs. reference 144.3

Note: the pre-fix rule as WRITTEN (sqrt on sigma, nothing on the
count) would have passed contracts 5 and 6 by accident - a
narrower sigma concentrates the same biomass into fewer cells,
which raises the peak. It fails contract 3, which is why that
contract is asserted separately rather than being folded into
the density checks.

70.5 Rollouts - and an uncomfortable result

E_perfg parameters (the live YAML state: default satiation
scale 0.8, porpoises 0.9, herring energy_content 7500),
3000 ticks, tick-zero = first tick with total biomass < 1e-3:

  policy   case      grid    porpoises  herring   gadoids
  greedy   pre-fix   16x16    t0=970    alive     alive
  greedy   fixed     16x16    t0=22     t0=2508   alive
  greedy   pre-fix   60x60    t0=1088   alive     alive
  greedy   fixed     60x60    t0=1088   alive     alive
  uniform  pre-fix   16x16    t0=19     alive     alive
  uniform  fixed     16x16    t0=17     alive     t0=2985
  uniform  pre-fix   60x60    t0=20     t0=2613   alive
  uniform  fixed     60x60    t0=20     t0=2613   alive

Two readings:

  * 60x60 is BIT-IDENTICAL before and after, in both brackets.
    That is the guarantee the early return at biomass_scale ==
    1.0 buys, and it means no 60x60 result in Sections 68-69
    needs revisiting.
  * On 16x16 the fix does NOT extend porpoise survival - in the
    greedy bracket it shortens it (970 -> 22). This is real and
    it is not a defect in the fix. The greedy forager never
    moves (pi_move = 0 by construction, see 68.6), and before
    the fix herring was smeared thinly over 251 of 256 cells, so
    a stationary porpoise always had SOMETHING in its own cell.
    After the fix the herring sits in 2 schools and the porpoise
    cells are empty. Ecologically the second world is the
    correct one - a porpoise should have to find the school -
    but it means the greedy bracket cannot measure this fix, for
    the same reason 69.6 records that it cannot measure the
    split fix.

Overlap at spawn, which is what actually decides the outcome
(share of porpoise biomass starting in a viable cell, seeds 1-5):

  pre-fix 16x16: viable cells [0,0,0,37,0]    overlap 0-11 %
  fixed   16x16: viable cells [15,14,18,19,10] overlap 0-9 %
  60x60 (both):  viable cells [177,175,182,330,151] overlap 0-12 %

So 16x16 is now STRUCTURALLY comparable to 60x60 - same viable
fraction, same overlap - and the remaining difference is
population size: 60x60 puts porpoises in 11-317 cells, 16x16 in
3-21. With three occupied cells the chance of any one of them
landing in a school is small, and there is no law of large
numbers to rescue it. That is a small-population artefact of the
1-ton biomass floor plus min_per_cell = 5 * min_split_biomass =
250 kg, not a density bug.

70.6 Practical recommendation

  (SUPERSEDED in part by Section 71.5: after this fix the
  density is grid-invariant, so the grid is no longer the
  binding constraint. 60x60 buys t0 = 205 against 16x16's 158 -
  worth having, but not a fix. The --n_eval_ticks advice below
  is also superseded: porpoises die at tick 158-205, well
  inside a 1000-tick horizon.)

  * Use --grid 60*60. It is the grid mareld2 is calibrated for
    and the only one where the porpoise population is large
    enough for the Section 69 margin to be sampled at all.
  * Raise --n_eval_ticks to 2000-3000. The best measured
    porpoise survival after Section 69 is 1088 ticks (60x60,
    greedy, 40 t), so at 1000 ticks the reward sees a dying
    species no matter what the policy does - there is no
    gradient to learn from.
  * --migration on weighs more heavily on a small grid: edge
    cells are 23 % of 16x16 against 6.6 % of 60x60.

70.7 Verification commands

  python3 -m pytest tests/ -q
    -> 128 passed, 0 failed  (104 before + 24 new).
  python3 -m py_compile lib/spawn/strategies.py
    lib/config/config_loader.py -> OK

70.8 What this does NOT fix

  * Porpoise spatial mismatch at spawn is unchanged and is now
    the binding constraint on small grids: colony n=10,
    sigma=3 for porpoises against n=25, sigma=1 for herring
    means most porpoise biomass starts away from the schools on
    EVERY grid (overlap 0-12 % even at 60x60). Open since
    Section 22, now quantified.
  * P1 seed_rate for DM species, retraining of the mareld2
    reference policies, and the starve_rate/growth_rate
    asymmetry all remain open exactly as listed in 69.6.
  * Nothing was committed.

================================================================
SECTION 71 - PORPOISES: THE PURSUIT RACE AND THE ACTION-
SIMPLEX ARTEFACT (diagnosis only - NO code changed)
================================================================

Trigger: a follow-up training run

  python3 train.py --project mareld2.yaml --run-name
    pzbpgp248nsrpfix7 --migration on --mortality off
    --generations inf --profile info --iter-per-gen 50
    --n_eval_ticks 1000 --n_deltas 16 --grid 16*16
    --worlds_refresh iteration --policynetwork 2 48 tanh
    --lr 0.03 --sigma 0.05 --visual

with porpoises respawned via env_driven so they start ON TOP of
the herring colonies. Porpoises still die almost immediately,
after nine generations, with mortality off.

Verdict: the run is not misconfigured and Sections 69/70 are
working. Porpoises now spawn essentially perfectly on the
herring (a_eff/a = 0.949 at tick 0) and the policy has learned
exactly the right thing (eat_frac 93.7 % -> 99.98 %, move_frac
5.05 % -> 0.02 %). They die anyway, because THE SCHOOL MOVES AND
THE PORPOISE CANNOT FOLLOW IT.

71.1 The structural cause

In _apply_movement (lib/environments/ecosystem.py):

  b_out = pi_move * B * dm_v

Movement is a PROPORTIONAL FOUR-WAY SPLIT of a continuous field,
not a directed translocation. There is no action meaning "move
the population one step". Therefore:

  * stay put  -> the school walks away from you;
  * move      -> 3 of 4 directions are worse than the cell you
                 are in, so every move dilutes the population.

Both paths end below u_X. It is a trap with no exit for the one
FG whose margin (s* - u_X = +0.064, Section 69) is too thin to
survive a halving of a_eff.

71.2 Measurements with the user's own checkpoints

All four policy_*.pth from results/pzbpgp248nsrpfix7 (including
obs_stats) loaded into the probe world, traced tick by tick.

As trained, 16x16:

  tick  B_porp  cells   s_x   pi_eat  herring   a_eff/a
                                      in cell
     0   2.844      7     -        -    36.3     0.949
     2   2.845     19  0.619    0.387   19.1     0.723
     5   2.822     25  0.540    0.818    4.66    0.505
    10   2.666     25  0.464    0.949    3.70    0.457
    40   0.242      5  0.406    0.972    3.01    0.451

They smear from 7 to 26 cells in the first five ticks and
a_eff/a halves from 0.95 to 0.44, against the 0.84 requirement.
After that s_x is permanently below u_X = 0.5 and they starve.
t_zero = 158.

With movement zeroed (pi_move := 0, same policy, 16x16):

  tick  B_porp  cells   s_x   herring in    a_eff/a
                              START cells
     1   2.845      7  0.680      311.3      0.925
     4   2.846      7  0.629      117.8      0.721
     8   2.846      7  0.561       57.6      0.443
    20   2.045      7  0.384       52.1      0.295
    58   0.000      0      -        5.65         -

The porpoises stay; the herring in their cells falls 311 -> 57 t
in eight ticks (-82 %) and they die at tick 58 - FASTER than if
they move.

  case            16x16      60x60
  as trained      t0 = 158   t0 = 205
  pi_move := 0    t0 =  57   t0 = 337

71.3 Herring is not the problem - it keeps its schools

This was the earlier suspicion and it is wrong:

  tick  herring   mean/cell   max/cell   cells >= 15 t
     0    853.3      3.33        81.4        18
    10    858.8      3.35       187.8        12
    40    866.1      3.38       120.8         9

Herring grows slightly and CONCENTRATES harder. But the schools
change cell: herring runs move_frac = 67 % in the user's log.
The structure survives, it just relocates.

71.4 The quantitative core

Section 69 requirement: a_eff/a >= 0.84. With
a*h = 0.05*20 = 1 that is 5.3 t of VISIBLE herring per cell, and
with herring visibility_floor = 0.35, 15.1 t TOTAL herring per
cell.

Mean density is 853.3/256 = 3.33 t/cell at 16x16 and
12000/3600 = 3.33 t/cell at 60x60 - IDENTICAL (that is Section
70 working). So the porpoise must permanently occupy cells at
4.5x the mean density, i.e. the top 4-7 % of the map, and must
follow them when they move.

71.5 Correction to 70.6

Since density is now grid-invariant, the grid is no longer the
binding constraint: 60x60 buys 205 ticks against 158. The
earlier "run 60x60" recommendation is not wrong, but it is not
a fix. Likewise --n_eval_ticks 1000 is NOT the problem in this
diagnosis - death occurs at tick 158-205.

71.6 ARS is not the problem either

From results/pzbpgp248nsrpfix7/biomass.jsonl:

  gen   eat%    move%   avg_ratio  rew_mean  rew_std
    1  93.68    5.05      0.034     -11.67     0.87
    3  99.53    0.44      0.136     -10.43     0.97
    5  99.90    0.10      0.149     -10.46     0.88
    9  99.98    0.02      0.066     -11.25     1.21

The gradient exists (rew_std 0.87-1.5), learning works, and the
policy converges on the optimum it CAN reach: always eat, never
move. bh = 0 in 438 of 438 evaluations, starvation = 1.0,
impact = 0.0 (so --mortality off was never relevant).
move_frac -> 0.02 % is not a failure, it is the right answer to
the wrong problem.

71.7 What "dilution" means, precisely

pi_move is four separate probabilities (N/E/S/W) out of the
softmax over 11 actions. Each direction gets its OWN share of
the cell's biomass, delivered to four DIFFERENT neighbours. A
cell with B = 0.6 t and pi_rest 0.1 / pi_eat 0.5 /
pi_move [.1,.1,.1,.1] keeps 0.36 t and ships 0.06 t to each of
four neighbours. Mass is conserved; it now sits in five cells.
Measured: 7 cells -> 26 cells in five ticks at constant total.

Three reasons it kills the porpoise:
  1. a_eff depends on PREY density in the cell, and 3 of 4
     directions are by definition worse than the best cell.
  2. Even the fraction that stays loses value, because the
     herring moves away.
  3. The extinction sweep bites the tails: split-off crumbs
     below extinction_threshold_factor * min_split_biomass are
     zeroed, so mass sent in a bad direction does not come back
     (mitigated but not removed by the Section 69 suppression).

Note this is NOT an arithmetic bug. Proportional four-way spread
is the correct description of a genuinely diffuse population
(phyto, zooplankton). The mismatch is that the SAME
representation is used for a schooling apex predator with ~26
individuals in the whole world.

71.8 One-hot is representable, and it is not enough

The user's objection is correct on its own terms: pi_move =
[1,0,0,0] IS representable (softmax logits are unbounded, and
--profile info sets argmax_penalty = 0.0, entropy_coef = 0.0,
temp_start = temp_end = 1.0, so nothing penalises a sharp
distribution). And cost depends only on pi_move_sum, not on how
the mass is spread:

  m_move = B * pi_move_sum * rm * cost_move * cost_factor

so [1,0,0,0] and [.25,.25,.25,.25] cost the SAME and one-hot
delivers to one cell instead of four. One-hot is strictly better
per MJ spent. (Earlier framing of coherent_movement as a COST
relief was wrong - it is a DELIVERY relief.)

But move, rest and eat live on the SAME simplex, so
pi_move_sum = 1 implies pi_eat = 0: a coherent move is not
"relocate", it is "starve for one tick and relocate". And
_apply_predation runs BEFORE _apply_movement in step(), so the
mass that moves does not eat in the destination cell either.

Sustainable move duty cycle = net_eat / (net_eat + move_cost),
computed on the live runtime matrices (max_intake_mat *
energy_gain_mat, 60x60):

  FG            sat  h(u_X)  net_eat  move   eat:move  duty   speed  cells/
                                      cost   ticks     max           tick
  zooplankton  0.80  0.375   +116.8    4.2    0.04    0.965  0.02   0.019
  gadoids      0.80  0.375    +79.5   12.0    0.15    0.869  0.25   0.217
  pelagic_fish 0.80  0.375    +38.8   12.0    0.31    0.764  1.0    0.764
  porpoises    0.90  0.444    +24.0  144.0    6.00    0.143  1.0    0.143

The porpoise must eat SIX ticks to pay for ONE move tick. Its
sustainable search speed is 0.143 cells/tick; herring's budget
is 0.764 cells/tick and its log shows move_frac = 67 %, i.e. it
is essentially at its ceiling. FACTOR 5.3 AGAINST. No policy in
the action space - one-hot or not - can follow a school moving
five times faster than the pursuer.

Search radius: from s_x = u_X the reserve funds
0.5 * 2800 / 144 = 9.7 pure move ticks. With 4-7 % viable cells
the mean distance to the nearest viable cell is 2-3 cells, i.e.
14-21 ticks of travelling at 0.143 cells/tick - the entire
reserve. Even a STATIONARY prey field is effectively out of
reach.

Conclusion: this is not a learning problem. The optimal
representable policy dies too. Learning difficulty is secondary
but real (the optimal policy today is a duty cycle with
hysteresis conditioned on s_x; ARS perturbs weights globally, so
a delta sharpening the move logits sharpens them in all 3600
cells, where the cost is immediate and the gain deferred and
diluted through the integral reward - expected value negative,
which is exactly the observed move_frac 5.05 % -> 0.02 %).

71.9 Proposed fix A: separate the movement head from the
     activity simplex

Today _calculate_decisions builds num_actions = 5 + N_all logits
and runs ONE softmax:

  index   0    1    2    3     4      5 ... 5+N_all-1
         mvN  mvE  mvS  mvW   rest   eat_prey_0 ... eat_prey_k

so pi_move_sum + pi_rest + pi_eat_sum == 1 per cell.

Proposal: two independent distributions, two softmaxes.

  head A (activity):    1 + N_all logits -> pi_rest, pi_eat[..]
  head B (relocation):  1 + 4    logits -> pi_stay, pi_move[4]

out_dim goes from 5 + N_all to 6 + N_all - ONE extra logit, not
a new architecture. Semantics: every ton makes an activity
choice AND a relocation choice in the same tick. A porpoise
swims and echolocates during the same six-hour tick; making
those mutually exclusive is a modelling artefact, not biology.

Cost becomes ADDITIVE instead of a shared share:

  m_act  = B * (pi_rest*cost_rest + pi_eat_sum*cost_eat)
                * rm * cost_factor
  m_relo = B * pi_move_sum * cost_move * rm * cost_factor

b_out = pi_move * B * dm_v is unchanged, and _apply_predation
needs no change at all - pi_eat is merely normalised
differently.

New sustainable share:

  h(u_X)*gain > rm * (cost_eat + p_move*cost_move)
  150.0       > 126 + 144 * p_move    ->  p_move < 0.167

i.e. 0.143 -> 0.167 under the NAIVE (absolute) reading of
movement_cost. Only +17 %. What actually changes is (a)
movement stops being a sawtooth drop in the reserve - ds becomes
a smooth function of p_move, positive everywhere below the
ceiling - and therefore (b) the optimal policy becomes a
CONSTANT p_move instead of a time-varying duty cycle, which lies
in a flat direction ARS can find with a single delta.

71.10 The three decisions this forces (must be settled BEFORE
      any code is written)

Surfaced by the test case "rest = 1, move = [0.5, 0.5, 0, 0]",
which under separation means: relocate the whole population
without foraging, splitting it N/E. Well-defined - passive
transit - and it exposes three hidden choices.

DECISION 1: is movement_cost ABSOLUTE or an INCREMENT over
rest? Today move = 1 implies rest = eat = 0, so a pure move tick
costs 90*1.6 = 144. Under separation the activity head always
sums to 1, so you always pay a baseline on top: 234 MJ/ton.
Physiologically the increment reading is correct
(FMR = RMR + activity surcharge), and it dominates the result:

  reading      cost_move   eat+move cost   sustainable p_move
  absolute        1.6      90*(1.4+1.6)=270   24/144 = 0.167
  increment       0.6      90*(1.4+0.6)=180   24/54  = 0.444

0.444 cells/tick against herring's 0.764 is a factor 1.7
against, not 5.3. THE REAL GAIN OF SEPARATION SITS HERE, not in
the 0.143 -> 0.167 computed in 71.9.

DECISION 2: hidden_frac = pi_rest, or pi_rest * pi_stay? In
_apply_predation the hidden share is exactly pi_rest:

  visible_frac = 1 - pi_rest * (1 - visibility_floor)

Today "hide" and "flee" are mutually exclusive on one simplex.
Separated, a prey FG gets rest = 1 plus a move distribution =
maximum invisibility AND full mobility in the same tick, at the
price of zero intake. That re-introduces the "perfect hide"
attractor visibility_floor was built to break, in a mobile
variant. Fix is one line - hidden_frac = pi_rest * pi_stay
(whoever swims is seen) - but it MUST ship in the same change,
otherwise separation is a net regression for the herring
balance.

DECISION 3: should coherent_movement force one-hot on the
RELOCATION head? Separation makes movement payable; it does not
make it concentrated ([0.5,0.5,0,0] still fragments). The
one-hot collapse already exists in _calculate_decisions for
cells below min_split_biomass; the change is to OR in a per-FG
flag. See 71.11.

71.11 Proposed fix B: coherent_movement per FG

The mechanism already exists. In _calculate_decisions, cells
with 0 < B < dm_min_split have their distribution collapsed to
a one-hot argmax, precisely so small groups migrate as a unit.
For porpoises min_split_biomass = 50.0 kg = 0.05 t while the
occupied cells hold 0.27-0.62 t, so the collapse NEVER fires for
them (verified: all sub-threshold = False).

Four small places:
  1. fg_library.yaml: coherent_movement: true on porpoises.
  2. functional_group.py: read it in __init__ like
     satiation_scale (a flag, no unit conversion).
  3. ecosystem._build_static_caches: cache dm_coherent next to
     dm_min_split.
  4. _calculate_decisions: widen the mask to
     sub_thr | (B_dm > 0) & dm_coherent[:, None, None].
     The argmax / one_hot / np.where block is unchanged.
     ~12 lines total.

Cheap proxy (no code): set porpoise min_split_biomass to
1000-2000 kg so every cell is sub-threshold. But
_dm_split_thr = min_split_biomass * extinction_threshold_factor
would move the sweep from 25 kg to 500 kg and wipe the
population, so extinction_threshold_factor must drop to ~0.02 in
the same test - which mixes two effects in one experiment. That
is why fix B proper is the clean variant: min_split_biomass
keeps meaning "indivisible unit / extinction floor",
coherent_movement means "moves as a school".

Costs of A + B: all checkpoints fall (already invalid since the
Holling fix); move_frac + rest_frac + eat_frac no longer sum to
1, so move_frac becomes an independent number in [0,1] and
ent_cell must become two entropies - the 99.98 % eat_frac from
pzbpgp248nsrpfix7 will not be comparable; argmax_penalty and
entropy_coef must be computed per head; move masking
(full_mask[:, 0:4]) moves to the relocation block and pi_stay
must always be valid (it replaces "no legal action -> rest");
out_dim must be updated in train.py / trainer.py /
parallel_worker.py.

71.12 Proposed fix C: visibility_floor as a (predator, prey)
      property

This is the one that lowers the REQUIREMENT rather than raising
the capability, and it has the strongest literature argument in
the whole chain. Herring's 0.35 turns the 5.3 t visible/cell
requirement into 15.1 t total/cell. visibility_floor models
VISUAL refuge; a porpoise uses high-frequency biosonar, not
sight. With full visibility for porpoise->herring the
requirement drops back to 5.3 t/cell, which the school edges
deliver - multiplying the number of viable cells and shrinking
the distance that has to be travelled. It is a single matrix
value.

Recommended order: C first (lowers the requirement), then A
(makes movement payable and learnable), then B (makes it
concentrated). A alone does not win the pursuit race.

71.13 Reproduction

Scratch diagnostics used for this section (not committed, they
live only in the session scratch dir - recreate as needed):
  porp_ckpt.py   - loads results/<run>/policy_*.pth into
                   train._ProbeEnvBuilder and traces B, cells,
                   s_x, pi_eat/pi_move/pi_rest, herring in cell
                   and a_eff/a per tick.
  porp_trace.py  - same trace on the live YAML state with the
                   uniform/greedy brackets plus per-stage loss
                   attribution from ab_test_hunger_energy.
  policy_gate.py - p_eat_min / p_move_max per DM from the live
                   max_intake_mat * energy_gain_mat; this is
                   where the 71.8 table comes from.

71.14 Status

  * NOTHING was changed in this section. No code, no YAML, no
    tests. Diagnosis only. Test suite is unchanged at 128
    passed.
  * Noted in passing: phytoplankton.seed_rate has been set from
    1e-07 to 0.0 in the working copy. That removes the
    recolonisation floor at the base of the food web. Not the
    porpoise cause, but it is exactly the P1 item behind "prey
    never recovers" and it will bite beyond ~1000 ticks.
  * Still open, unchanged from 69.6 / 70.8: P1 seed_rate for DM
    species, retraining of the mareld2 reference policies, the
    starve_rate/growth_rate asymmetry, spatial mismatch at
    spawn, and the GUI validator for
    handling_time != 1/max_intake_rate.
  * Next action requires a user decision: pick C, A or B (and,
    if A, settle Decisions 1-3 in 71.10 first).

72. Fix C implemented: visibility_floor per (predator, prey)

Section 71.12 proposed it, the user approved "the whole chain".
This section is the implementation record. Fixes A (separate
movement head) and B (coherent_movement per FG) are NOT done and
remain open exactly as described in 71.9 / 71.11.

72.1 Data model

  visibility_floor is now resolvable at TWO levels:

    1. species_definitions[<fg>].visibility_floor  - the prey's
       own default, unchanged meaning, unchanged values
       (zoo 0.2, pelagic_fish 0.35, gadoids 0.6, rest 0.0).
    2. interaction_definitions[<pred>_preys_on_<prey>]
       .visibility_floor - OPTIONAL override for that pair only.
       Absent / empty = inherit level 1.

  No new YAML key namespace, no config_loader change: the
  _preys_on_ branch already copies the whole idef into
  params['interaction'][iid], so the engine had the value in
  hand already.

  The one concrete calibration change:

    porpoises_preys_on_pelagic_fish.visibility_floor: 1.0

  Rationale in the YAML comment (Au 1993; Verfuss et al. 2009):
  biosonar is not defeated by a visual/schooling refuge. Effect
  on the 71.8 arithmetic: required total herring per cell drops
  from 15.1 t to 5.3 t, i.e. the school edges now qualify and the
  number of viable cells multiplies.

72.2 Engine (lib/environments/ecosystem.py)

  _build_static_caches:
    * vis_floor_mat, shape (N_dm, N_all). Base = the column FG's
      own floor broadcast over all rows; overridden per cell
      where the interaction carries an explicit value (clipped
      to [0, 1]).
    * _has_pair_vis_floor - False when every cell equals the
      column default. Both the predation and the observation
      path then take the OLD vector code, so legacy projects are
      bit-identical and pay nothing.
    * _obs_vis_floor - per-DM view of vis_floor_mat aligned with
      the compact obs_others_idx layout.
    * dm_visibility_floor / _all_visibility_floor are kept; they
      are still the base of the matrix.

  _apply_predation:
    * With a pair override the visible prey biomass becomes a
      (N_dm, N_all, H, W) tensor and feeds Holling saturation
      (Bp) directly.
    * Per-predator clamp: D = min(D, B_prey_pair_visible). Needed
      because the shared availability cap is now the MAX over
      predators that actually prey on j - without the clamp a
      floor-0 predator could eat the hidden fraction just because
      a better-detecting predator raised the cap.
    * The shared cap is masked by eat_static_mask so non-predator
      rows cannot inflate it. Reduces exactly to the legacy
      vector when all rows share the column default.

  _build_observation_batch:
    * When a pair override exists the visible-biomass stack is
      built per observer inside the DM loop instead of once
      globally. "What I can see" and "what I can eat" therefore
      stay consistent per predator; for non-prey columns the
      observer still falls back to the prey's own scalar.

  Checkpoints: in_dim is unchanged, so existing .pth files load.
  The observation SCALE changes for affected pairs, so retraining
  the mareld2 reference policies is recommended (not forced).

72.3 GUI (fgconfig/fgconfig.py)

  * New fifth matrix on the FG Interactions tab: "Visibility
    Floor (min visible fraction of column to row)", stored on the
    existing {row}_preys_on_{col} key, so apply_matrix_changes
    persists it generically.
  * New cell_type "unit_optional": default is the EMPTY string,
    not 1.0. That was the critical detail - reusing unit_slider
    would have materialised 48 explicit values and silently
    killed the per-FG default. Live validation accepts "" or
    0.0 <= v <= 1.0.
  * apply_matrix_changes: for data_key == visibility_floor an
    emptied cell now pop()s the key, so clearing a cell really
    restores inheritance. Other data_keys keep the legacy
    "empty string leaves the stored value alone" behaviour.
  * Gated on preys_on exactly like assimilation_factor and
    handling_time (cell greys out when predation is off).
  * A muted explanatory label under the matrix states the
    inheritance rule.
  * FG editor label renamed to "Visibility Floor (default;
    overridable per predator)" so it is clear the matrix wins.

72.4 Tests

  * tests/test_spawn_grid_invariance.py no longer hardcodes
    HERRING_VISIBILITY_FLOOR = 0.35. It resolves the
    (porpoises, pelagic_fish) value from fg_library.yaml with the
    same inheritance rule as the engine; otherwise the Section 70
    viability assertions would have silently measured the wrong
    predator.
  * tests/test_visibility_floor_pairs.py (new, 10 tests). Seven
    contracts: inheritance, legacy vector path preserved,
    override is pair-local, clipping to [0, 1], hidden prey is
    eaten only by the detecting predator in the same tick and
    cell, no over-harvest under the shared cap, floor is inert
    when nobody hides, observation attenuates per observer, and
    the live library really carries the biosonar override.
  * Suite: 128 -> 138 passed. Smoke test
    "--species porpoises --generations 1 --iter-per-gen 1
    --workers 1" completes and checkpoints.

72.5 Status / next

  * C is done. A (71.9, needs Decisions 1-3 in 71.10 settled
    first) and B (71.11) are untouched and still the candidates
    if porpoises keep losing the pursuit race.
  * NOT re-run yet: a full-length porpoise rollout to confirm
    that the extinction at tick 158-205 is actually gone. That is
    the natural next measurement now that the requirement is
    5.3 t/cell.
  * Unchanged open items from 71.14: phytoplankton.seed_rate is
    0.0 in the working copy, P1 seed_rate for DM species,
    retraining of the mareld2 reference policies, the
    starve_rate/growth_rate asymmetry, spatial mismatch at spawn,
    and the GUI validator for handling_time != 1/max_intake_rate.
  * NOTE (added in 73): the pair floor was subsequently tuned to
    0.95 instead of 1.0 - a small residual advantage is left to
    schooling, so the break-even requirement is ~5.6 t/cell
    rather than 5.3. tests/test_visibility_floor_pairs.py no
    longer pins the exact magnitude, only that the override
    exists and is far above the herring default of 0.35.


73 THE ABSORBING ZERO AT THE BASE OF THE FOOD WEB
  (phytoplankton extinction; Holling III is necessary but not
   sufficient)

  Trigger: the user's hypothesis that phytoplankton extinction
  should already be prevented by the Holling values - "when phyto
  gets too sparse, zooplankton no longer detects it and it can
  regrow". The hypothesis is correct AND already implemented, but
  it only covers the continuous case. This section documents the
  discrete case it does not cover, and the two fixes applied.

73.1 Holling type III is already on, for the right pair

  _build_static_caches enables type III automatically for every
  predator with EXACTLY ONE prey on its menu:

    n_prey_per_pred = eat_static.sum(axis=1)
    self._type3_pred_mask = (n_prey_per_pred == 1)

  Zooplankton is the only true specialist in fg_library.yaml, so:

    zooplankton -> phytoplankton : type III
    everything else (generalists): type II

  With a = 0.25 and h = 4.0 (a*h = 1) the per-capita grazing
  f(P)/P is approximately 0.25*P at low P and therefore vanishes
  faster than linearly - exactly the "no longer detected" effect.
  Phytoplankton growth is linear in P (r = 0.0375). So
  mathematically phytoplankton CANNOT be grazed to zero
  asymptotically in a cell where P > 0.

73.2 But the refuge level is indistinguishable from extinction

  Equilibrium where logistic growth meets grazing (K = 24):

    Z per cell   stable P    fraction of K
       1.0 t     0.153 t        0.6 %
       2.4 t     0.063 t       0.26 %
       5.0 t     0.030 t       0.13 %
       9.7 t     0.016 t       0.06 %
      20.0 t     0.0075 t      0.03 %

  mareld2 starts zooplankton at 8750-35000 t over 3600 cells,
  i.e. 2.4-9.7 t/cell. The refuge therefore sits at a per mille
  of carrying capacity: not zero, but functionally collapsed and
  a flat line along the x-axis in the biomass plot. It is also
  the ONLY equilibrium - at those Z the net is negative for every
  P above it, so the system is pulled there.

73.3 The actual hole: one tick can zero a cell EXACTLY

  _apply_predation capped demand as

    scale = where(total_demand > B_prey_visible,
                  B_prey_visible / (total_demand + 1e-9), 1.0)

  leaving a residue of B * 1e-9 / (D + 1e-9). Relative precision
  in float32 is ~1.2e-7, i.e. LARGER than that residue, so
  B_old - intake rounds to EXACTLY 0.0. Verified numerically
  (B = 5, D = 9.6 -> leftover 0.0, == 0.0 is True).

  When does overharvest happen? D = Z * f(P), so
  D/P = a*Z*P / (1 + a*h*P^2), maximised at P = 1 with value
  0.125*Z. Any cell with Z >~ 8 t can zero its phytoplankton in a
  single tick - inside mareld2's start range. Trajectory traces
  (a = 0.25, h = 4, start P = 5.0, legacy zeroing):

    Z =  5.0  overharvest ticks   0   never zeroed
    Z =  9.0  5.00 -> 2.93 -> 0.95 -> 0   zeroed at tick 3
    Z = 14.0  5.00 -> 1.69 -> 0            zeroed at tick 2
    Z = 20.0  5.00 -> 0.199 -> 0.0085      never zeroed
    Z = 40.0  5.00 -> 0                    zeroed at tick 1

  Note Z = 20 skips the dangerous band in one jump and survives
  while Z = 9 and Z = 14 land inside it - the outcome is not
  monotone in grazing pressure, which is why this was never
  obvious from the aggregate curves.

  The zeroing happens from a HEALTHY P (1-5 t), jumping straight
  past the low-density region where the type III refuge would
  have bitten. After that, zero is absorbing:

    * growth = r*P*(1 - P/K) is multiplicative in P,
    * movement_speed = 0.0, so no diffusion from neighbours,
    * seed_rate was 0.0 in the working copy.

  Type III is multiplicative in P and can by construction never
  restore anything from zero. The refuge protects against
  ASYMPTOTIC grazing-down, not against a discrete zeroing.

  Two mechanisms are innocent here and were checked:
  _apply_extinction_threshold requires min_split_biomass > 0,
  which phytoplankton does not have; and visibility_floor is
  irrelevant because NDMs never hide (hidden_frac = 0).

73.4 Fix 1 - phytoplankton.seed_rate restored

  fgconfig/fg_library.yaml: phytoplankton.seed_rate 0.0 ->
  1e-07, i.e. 2.4e-6 t/cell/tick. It is the only mechanism that
  can lift a cell off exact zero. Section 69.6 states it plainly:
  "Without it, any retraining teaches the policies to live in a
  dying world."

  Caveat kept in the YAML comment: this is a PERSISTENCE floor,
  not a fast recovery. Reaching the refuge level (~0.015 t/cell)
  from zero by seeding alone is on the order of 6000 ticks.
  benthic_community uses 1e-06, ten times more.

73.5 Fix 2 - relative harvest cap in _apply_predation

  New module-level constant in lib/environments/ecosystem.py:

    MAX_HARVEST_FRAC = np.float32(0.999)

  and the cap became

    B_harvestable = B_prey_visible * MAX_HARVEST_FRAC
    denom = np.maximum(total_demand, 1e-30)
    scale = where(total_demand > B_harvestable,
                  B_harvestable / denom, 1.0)

  The margin is relative, so it is 5 orders of magnitude above
  float32 epsilon and 0.1 % below anything ecologically
  meaningful. The 1e-30 guards the division only; the branch
  decides when it runs, so it cannot leak into the
  total_demand == 0 case. No checkpoint impact (no dimension or
  observation-scale change).

  Deliberate consequence: the branch now also triggers in the
  narrow window between 0.999*B_vis and B_vis, so a full harvest
  of a cell is 0.1 % smaller than before. That is the fix, not a
  side effect.

73.6 Measurement

  Worst-case probe: mareld2 FG set, 16x16 grid, 1000 ticks,
  zooplankton pinned at constant biomass with an empty reserve
  (hunger gate fully open, pi_eat = 1 on phytoplankton), phyto
  initialised U(1, 6) t/cell. Cell-ticks where predation left
  EXACTLY 0.0, measured BEFORE _apply_growth - measuring after
  growth shows nothing, because seed_rate lifts the cell off zero
  again and hides the event:

    Z per cell   cap 1.0 (legacy)   cap 0.999   final mean
       9.0 t          158                0        0.0162
      14.0 t          233                0        0.0105
      20.0 t          243                0        0.0074

  So the zeroing was pervasive - 60-95 % of the 256 cells - and
  it is now gone. The final mean is IDENTICAL in both columns,
  which is the point: the fix removes the discrete zeroing
  without moving the ecology. The final levels also match the
  73.2 table, confirming the type III refuge is where the system
  settles once zero is unreachable.

73.7 Tests

  tests/test_absorbing_zero.py (new, 10 tests):
    * overharvest never reaches exactly zero (the regression -
      verified to fail with MAX_HARVEST_FRAC patched to 1.0,
      leftover 0.0),
    * the removal equals MAX_HARVEST_FRAC of the visible biomass,
    * the cap is tight (>= 99 % still harvestable), so it is not
      a stealth nerf of predation,
    * under-demand is untouched: scale stays exactly 1.0,
    * the hidden (rested) fraction is still fully protected,
    * 200 consecutive overharvest ticks never reach zero,
    * the type III refuge is reachable after the fix,
    * phytoplankton.seed_rate > 0 in the live library,
    * a zeroed NDM cell recovers with seed_rate, and stays at
      zero with seed_rate = 0 (opt-in preserved).

  Suite: 138 -> 148 passed. Smoke test "--species zooplankton
  --generations 1 --iter-per-gen 1 --workers 1" completes and
  checkpoints.

73.8 Rejected / deferred

  * Raising zooplankton handling_time above 4.0 would lower the
    saturation ceiling 1/h and thus the D/P peak, but it
    collides with the calibration rationale in fg_library.yaml
    (h = 1/a so the ceiling coincides with the physiological
    maximum). That is a calibration discussion, not a fix.
  * seed_rate for DM species (P1 from 69.6/70.8) is still open.
    The predation cap now guarantees DMs are not zeroed by
    predation either, but starvation and the extinction
    threshold can still zero them, and those paths have no
    recolonisation floor.

73.9 Status / next

  * Both fixes are in and measured. The open item "phytoplankton
    .seed_rate is 0.0 in the working copy" from 71.14/72.5 is
    closed.
  * Still not run: the full-length porpoise rollout from 72.5,
    and the retraining of the mareld2 reference policies. Both
    fixes are prerequisites for that retraining being meaningful
    rather than a study of a dying world.

74 THE PORPOISE DIES BECAUSE THE SCHOOLS DISSOLVE
   (post-fix-C rollout; Section 71's diagnosis is CORRECTED here)

  Trigger: the user ran a full generation after fix C with
  --mortality on:

    train.py --project mareld2.yaml --run-name pzbpgp248nsrpfix9
      --migration on --mortality on --generations inf
      --profile info --iter-per-gen 50 --n_eval_ticks 1000
      --n_deltas 16 --grid 16*16 --worlds_refresh iteration
      --policynetwork 2 48 tanh --lr 0.03 --sigma 0.05 --visual

  and reported that the porpoises STILL converge to ~100 % eat
  and still go extinct. They do. This section explains why, and
  it overturns the core of Section 71: the cause is NOT the
  pursuit race, NOT the shared action simplex and NOT the
  movement cost. Fixes A and B as scoped in 71.9-71.11 would
  not have changed the outcome. Diagnosis only - no code, no
  YAML and no tests were changed in this section.

74.1 Correction to 71.4: the "0.84" is SATURATION, not a_eff/a

  Section 71.4 reads the Section 69 requirement as
  "a_eff/a >= 0.84". That is the wrong quantity, and it moves in
  the wrong direction: a_eff = a/(1 + a*h*B_vis) DECREASES with
  prey density, so a_eff/a >= 0.84 would require B_vis <= 0.19
  t/cell, i.e. prey SCARCITY. The quantity that matters is the
  Holling saturation

    sat = f/f_max = a*h*B_vis / (1 + a*h*B_vis)

  which is 0.84 at B_vis = 5.25 t/cell when a*h = 1. The 5.3
  t/cell figure in 71.4 is therefore right, but the formula
  attached to it was not. Any future probe must report sat, not
  a_eff/a.

74.2 The break-even identity, and the porpoise as an outlier

  A DM that eats 100 % of the time, at the reserve level where
  it exactly breaks even (s_x = u_x), gains

    gain = a * sat * h(u_x) * energy_gain_per_tonne  [MJ/t/tick]
    h(u_x) = max(0, 1 - u_x / satiation_scale)

  and pays cost = resting_metabolism * feeding_cost. So

    sat_min   = cost / (a * energy_gain_per_tonne * h(u_x))
    B_vis_min = sat_min / ((1 - sat_min) * a * h)

  Evaluated on the live mareld2 runtime matrices:

   predator      prey          h(u)  cost  a*gain sat_min need_vis
   gadoids       benthic       0.375  10.5   84.0  0.333   0.500
   gadoids       pelagic_fish  0.375  10.5  240.0  0.117   0.132
   pelagic_fish  zooplankton   0.375  15.2  144.0  0.281   0.392
   porpoises     gadoids       0.444 126.0  231.0  1.227   INFEAS
   porpoises     pelagic_fish  0.444 126.0  337.5  0.840   5.250
   zooplankton   phytoplankton 0.375   5.1  325.0  0.042   0.043

  Two things stand out.

  (a) The porpoise requirement is 5.25 t/cell against 0.04-0.50
      for every other pair - a factor 10 to 130. The reason is
      structural: rm*feeding_cost = 90*1.4 = 126 MJ/t/tick is
      8-25x the fish values (correct for an endotherm), while
      a*energy_gain = 337.5 is only 1.4-2.3x theirs. Metabolism
      was scaled up for a mammal; max_intake_rate was not.

  (b) porpoises -> gadoids has sat_min = 1.227 > 1, i.e. it is
      ARITHMETICALLY INFEASIBLE: no cod density whatsoever pays
      for a porpoise, because the hunger gate caps realised
      intake below the feeding cost. One of the porpoise's two
      menu items is pure loss at every density. This is a
      calibration error independent of everything else in this
      section and should be fixed or the item removed.

74.3 It is not a pursuit problem: the oracle also dies

  Decisive experiment (porp_pinned.py). Porpoise actions forced
  to "eat 100 %, never move"; every other DM keeps its trained
  policy, so the herring field is untouched. Three variants:

    spread   keep the cells the trained policy ends up in
    pinned   all biomass concentrated in the 5 richest herring
             cells at t=0, held there
    perfect  re-concentrated onto the CURRENT 5 richest herring
             cells EVERY tick - an oracle pursuer with free,
             lossless, instantaneous movement

    mode      died at tick
    spread        380
    pinned        366
    perfect       598

  The oracle dies too. Free movement buys 218 ticks and nothing
  more. Since fixes A and B can at best approximate the oracle,
  neither can save the porpoise. 71.9-71.11 are hereby demoted
  from "the fix" to "unrelated improvements".

74.4 The real cause: the herring field flattens to below the
     requirement

  herring_field.py runs the same world with the porpoises
  REMOVED entirely, so the herring field is not being cropped by
  them, and counts cells at or above 5.25 t/cell:

   tick  B_pf   max   top5  top20  cells>=5.25  mean
      0  853.3 81.41  57.47  35.19      33      3.333
     50  848.8 24.72  22.17  16.89      51      3.316
    100  836.6 14.64  13.94  11.85      51      3.268
    200  790.2  8.56   8.13   6.80      22      3.087
    300  716.5  4.97   4.77   4.50       0      2.799
    500  551.4  3.63   3.60   3.47       0      2.154
   1000  245.9  2.27   2.19   1.97       0      0.960

  The porpoise habitat ceases to exist at tick ~300, with zero
  porpoises present. Everything after that is bookkeeping.

  And the mechanism is not stock depletion. With --mortality off
  the herring stock is fully preserved (855 t at tick 1000,
  against 853 at t=0) and the porpoises STILL die, at tick 453.
  What kills them is the SHAPE of the field, not its mass:

    mean herring density = 853.3 / 256 = 3.33 t/cell
    porpoise requirement =                5.25 t/cell

  A SPATIALLY UNIFORM HERRING FIELD CANNOT SUPPORT A PORPOISE
  AT ALL, at any stock size. The porpoise is entirely dependent
  on patchiness: it needs cells at >= 1.6x the mean, permanently,
  and it needs them to still be there next tick.

  The initial spawn delivers that patchiness (max 81 t/cell, 24x
  the mean). It then decays away: herring runs pi_move ~ 0.085
  per tick spread over four directions, which is a diffusion
  process, and diffusion relaxes any initial peak toward the
  mean. max/mean goes 24.4 (t=0) -> 7.5 (t=50) -> 4.5 (t=100)
  -> 1.8 (t=300) -> 2.4 (t=1000, on a collapsing mean). The
  schools do not relocate, they DISSOLVE.

  This is exactly the four-way dilution of 71.7 - but it bites
  the HERRING, not the porpoise. 71.7 looked at the right
  mechanism through the wrong end of the telescope.

74.5 Why ARS "converges to 100 % eat"

  Not a learning failure, and this time for a stronger reason
  than 71.6 gave. In results/pzbpgp248nsrpfix9/biomass.jsonl the
  porpoise ratio is bit-identical at 3.515625e-13 in 59 of 59
  evaluations - that is bh clamped at 1e-12 over b0 = 2.844.
  Every rollout of every delta of every iteration ends with the
  porpoises extinct. The survival component of the fitness is
  therefore EXACTLY FLAT: there is no gradient to climb. The
  only remaining signal is the biomass integral, which is
  maximised by eating, so ARS climbs that. It is answering the
  only question it is being asked.

  Fix C did work, measurably: death moved from tick 158-205 to
  tick 441 (trained policy, full trace), and avg_energy_ratio
  for the porpoises rose from 0.006 to 0.24 within one
  generation. It just cannot outrun a habitat that disappears.

  Worth noting from the same log: pelagic_fish move_frac
  collapsed from 62 % to 7 % over 50 iterations. The herring is
  learning to sit still - which slows the diffusion but does not
  stop it, and does not re-concentrate anything.

74.6 Recalibration only postpones; it does not rescue

  Forced-eat rollouts with single parameters overridden in
  memory (porp_pinned.py, spread variant):

    override                 need_vis t/cell   died at tick
    none (u=0.5, sat_s=0.9)      5.25              380
    satiation_scale 1.0          2.95              442
    maintenance_level 0.4+1.0    1.65              586
    maintenance_level 0.3        1.27              757
    max_intake_rate *1.6         1.11              925

  Every one of them helps, monotonically in need_vis, and every
  one of them still dies - because the herring field keeps
  falling and eventually crosses even 1.1 t/cell. Lowering the
  requirement cannot beat a field heading for zero. Sensitivity
  for the record (porpoises -> pelagic_fish):

    u_x  sat_scale  h(u)   sat_min  need_vis t/cell
    0.50   0.90     0.444   0.840        5.250
    0.50   1.00     0.500   0.747        2.947
    0.40   0.90     0.556   0.672        2.049
    0.30   0.90     0.667   0.560        1.273
    0.30   1.00     0.700   0.533        1.143

74.7 Secondary findings, all separable from the above

  * Realised demographic headroom. natural_mortality is applied
    PER TICK and over a 1000-tick evaluation the survivor
    fractions are: zooplankton 0.082, pelagic_fish 0.607,
    gadoids 0.819, porpoises 0.951. Zooplankton's anabolic
    ceiling is growth_rate * (satiation_scale - u_x) =
    0.01 * 0.3 = 0.003/tick against 0.0025/tick of mortality -
    a headroom of only 1.2. It is structurally near break-even
    before any predation is applied.
  * For the porpoise the same comparison explains the flat
    B = 2.72 plateau in the trace: realised q_x was ~ +0.04, so
    growth was 0.001*0.04 = 4e-5/tick against natural mortality
    5e-5/tick. Realised headroom < 1. With mortality on, the
    effective maintenance level is u_x + nm/growth_rate = 0.55,
    not 0.50, which lifts the requirement from 5.25 to ~24
    t/cell. THIS is the specific reason the user's --mortality
    on run looked no better than the earlier --mortality off
    runs despite fix C.
  * Herring starvation is real but not budget-driven: its
    sat_min is 0.281 (need 0.39 t/cell) against 9.8 t/cell of
    zooplankton available, and at its observed action mix
    (rest 0.53, eat 0.39, move 0.085) its net is +7 to +9
    MJ/t/tick. The 233 t of starvation loss in the 1000-tick
    probe comes from the heterogeneous tail of cells below u_x,
    not from an unpayable budget. Predation by gadoids is the
    larger term (299 t).
  * The extinction sweep fires 286 times for gadoids and 61
    times for pelagic_fish over 1000 ticks - the diffusion tail
    being zeroed, as predicted in 71.7 point 3.

74.8 What the next arc has to decide

  The porpoise is a SYMPTOM. The primary defect is that the
  model has no school-cohesion force for pelagic_fish: schooling
  exists only in the initial spawn and is erased by four-way
  proportional movement within ~300 ticks. Candidates, in the
  order the evidence supports:

  1. coherent_movement for PELAGIC_FISH (fix B from 71.11,
     retargeted). The mechanism already exists in
     _calculate_decisions as the sub-min_split_biomass one-hot
     collapse; it needs a per-FG flag. Preserves patchiness
     instead of manufacturing it. ~12 lines. Note 71.11 scoped
     this to porpoises, where 74.3 now shows it cannot help.
  2. An explicit aggregation / taxis term for schooling FGs, so
     patches re-form rather than merely failing to disperse.
     Larger change, needs a mathematical definition first, and
     Strategi.pdf does not currently specify one.
  3. Porpoise budget recalibration (74.6). Necessary but not
     sufficient - do it only together with 1 or 2, otherwise it
     just moves the death tick.
  4. Remove or repair the infeasible porpoises -> gadoids pair
     (74.2b). Independent of everything else, and cheap.

  Do NOT retrain the mareld2 reference policies before 1 or 2 is
  settled: with the field flattening as it does, retraining
  still teaches the policies a dying world, which is the same
  warning 69.6 gave about seed_rate.

74.9 Reproduction - the probes are COMMITTED this time

  Unlike 71.13, the diagnostics behind this section were kept.
  They live in tools/probes/ and resolve the repo root from
  __file__, so they run from any working directory:

    tools/probes/budget_gate.py
      No run needed, no policies loaded. Builds the live mareld2
      runtime matrices and prints the break-even table of 74.2
      plus the u_x / satiation_scale sensitivity of 74.6.
      Seconds to run. START HERE - it is the cheapest way to
      re-establish the whole argument.

    tools/probes/porp_ckpt9.py <run-name> [ticks]
      Tick-by-tick trace of the porpoises with the weights from
      results/<run-name>/: B, occupied cells, s_x, pi_eat /
      pi_move / pi_rest, herring in cell, visible herring.
      Default run pzbpgp248nsrpfix9, 400 ticks.

    tools/probes/porp_budget.py <run-name> [ticks]
      The same trace but as an ENERGY BUDGET: gain, cost, net
      (MJ/t/tick), hunger gate, Holling saturation, q_x = s_x -
      u_x. Use energy_level (= (R/B)/ME), NOT energy_reserve /
      max_energy_reserve - the first version of this probe got
      that wrong and reported s_x ~ 0.006 for a healthy animal.
      Ends with sat_min and the required visible density.

    tools/probes/porp_pinned.py {spread|pinned|perfect} [K]
      The decisive experiment of 74.3. Porpoise row of the
      decision tensor overridden to pure eating after the real
      _calculate_decisions has run, so every other DM keeps its
      trained policy and the herring field is untouched.
      Env vars: MORT=0 disables natural mortality, U_X, SAT_S
      and A_MULT override maintenance_level, satiation_scale and
      max_intake_rate in memory (A_MULT also divides
      handling_time, keeping a*h = 1 per the fg_library
      convention) - that is how the 74.6 table was produced.

    tools/probes/herring_field.py [--keep-porpoises]
      The 74.4 table. Removes the porpoises entirely, then
      counts cells >= 5.25 t/cell per tick and reports max /
      top5 / top20 / mean, followed by the per-FG loss
      attribution and the extinction-sweep event counts of 74.7.

  All of them use train._ProbeEnvBuilder with grid 16*16 and
  seed 20260530 so the world is deterministic and the numbers in
  74.2-74.7 are exactly reproducible. Runtime: budget_gate
  seconds, the 1000-tick rollouts 5-20 minutes each.
